予測

１. 11/16に内閣府から公表される 2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDPは、前期比▲0.1％（前期比年
率▲0.3％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成長になったと推計される。 
２. 高水準の企業収益を背景に設備投資が前期比 1.4％と大幅に増加したが、天候不順の影響
などから民間消費が前期比▲0.3％と減少したこと、原油を中心に輸入が大幅に増加し、
外需が成長率を大きく押し下げたことなどから、小幅ながら 4四半期ぶりのマイナス成
長になったとみられる。 
３. 名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率 2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質 GDPの伸
びを大きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％、前年比 2.5％と予測する。 
４. 2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時
的な影響が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回
復を妨げる可能性が高い。 
５. 現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むこ
となどから、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまる
と予想している。 
ニッセイ基礎研究所 2026-10-30

2026年 7-9月期の実質 GDP 
～前期比▲0.1％（年率▲0.3％）を予測～

経済研究部 経済調査部長 斎藤  太郎 
(03)3512-1836  tsaito@nli-research.co.jp

### Page 2

名目 GDPは前期比 0.5％（前期比年率 2.0％）と 10四半期連続で増加し、実質 GDPの伸びを大
きく上回るだろう。GDPデフレーターは前期比 0.6％（4-6月期：同 1.0％）、前年比 2.5％（4-6月
期：同 2.6％）と予測する。

なお、11/16に内閣府から 2026年 7-9月期の GDP速報が発表される際には、基礎統計の改定や季
節調整のかけ直しなどから、成長率が過去に遡って改定される。当研究所では、2026年 4-6月期の
実質 GDP成長率は、外需、設備投資が上方修正されることから、前期比年率 1.4％から同 2.3％へ
上方修正されると予想している。

2026年 7-9月期のマイナス成長の一因となった民間消費の減少は、天候不順による一時的な影響
が大きい。ただし、こうした影響が解消されても、物価上昇の加速が消費の回復を妨げる可能性が
高い。消費者物価上昇率は 2026年 9月に 9ヵ月ぶりの 2％台となったが、原材料価格の上昇を耐久
財や日用品などの販売価格に転嫁する動きが今後さらに広がることから、2026年度末にかけて 3％
台まで高まる公算が大きい。物価上昇率の高まりによる実質購買力の低下を背景に 2026年度後半
の民間消費は弱めの動きが続くことが予想される。 
現時点では、2026年 10-12月期はプラス成長に復帰するものの、民間消費が伸び悩むことなどか
ら、前期比年率 0％台半ばとマイナス成長の後としては低めの伸びにとどまると予想している。

1 10/30までに公表された経済指標をもとに予測している。今後公表される経済指標の結果によって予測値を修正する可
能性がある。

### Page 3

民間消費は前期比▲0.3％と予測する。 
実質賃金はプラスの伸びが続いているが、物価上昇率の高まりによって実質購買力が押し下げら
れていることに加え、台風や豪雨、長雨などの天候不順が個人消費を下押しした。 
2026年 7-9月期の消費関連指標を確認すると、延べ宿泊者数（うち日本人）は前期比 2.8％（4-6
月期：同▲0.9％）の増加となったが、自動車税の
「環境性能割」の廃止を受けて 4-6月期に前期比
10.7％の高い伸びとなった自動車販売台数が、その
反動で同▲7.5％と落ち込んだほか、外食産業売上
高が前期比▲0.3％（4-6月期：同 0.2％）、小売業
販売額指数が前期比▲2.1％（4-6月期：同 0.6％）
と減少した（いずれもニッセイ基礎研究所による
季節調整値、外食産業売上高、小売業販売額指数
は消費者物価指数で実質化）。

・住宅投資～弱めの動きが続く～

住宅投資は前期比▲0.2％と 2四半期連続の減
少を予測する。 
新設住宅着工戸数（季節調整済・年率換算値）
は、建築物省エネ法・建築基準法改正前の駆け
込み需要の反動で、2025年 1-3月期の 86.7万戸
から 4-6月期に 62.0万戸へ急減した後、7-9月
が 71.9万戸と持ち直し、その後は概ね 70万戸
台半ばで推移している。 
GDP統計の住宅投資は住宅価格上昇の影響も
あり弱めの動きとなっている。

・民間設備投資～高水準の企業収益を背景に増加～

民間設備投資は前期比 1.4％と 3四半期ぶりの増加を予測する。 
設備投資の一致指標である資本財総供給は 2026年 4-6月期の前期比 4.9％の後、7-9月期も同 4.9％
となった。また、機械投資の先行指標である機械受注（船舶・電力を除く民需）は 2026年 4-6月
期に前期比 0.2％と 3四半期連続で増加した後、7、8月の平均は 4-6月期を 0.4％上回っている。 
日銀短観 2026年 9月調査では、2026年度の設備投資計画（含むソフトウェア・研究開発投資額、
除く土地投資額）が 6月調査から 0.5％上方修正され、前年度比 9.3％（全規模・全産業）となった。 
設備投資は、高水準の企業収益を背景に、人手不足対応の省力化投資、デジタル化に向けた情報

・公的固定資本形成～2四半期連続の減少～

公的固定資本形成は、前期比▲2.4％と予測する。 
公共工事の先行指標である公共工事請負金額
は 2026年 7-9月期に前年比▲2.6％と 6四半期ぶ
りに減少した。また、公共工事の進捗を反映する
公共工事出来高（建設総合統計）は、2026年 4-6
月期に前年比 5.2％と 9四半期連続で増加した後、
2026年 7、8月の平均は同 2.4％と伸びが鈍化し
ている。 
2025年度補正予算による押し上げ効果が弱ま
っていることに加え、2026 年度当初予算の公共
事業関係費の伸びが抑えられていることから、弱
めの動きとなっている。

・外需～4四半期ぶりのマイナス寄与～

外需寄与度は前期比▲0.4％（前期比年率▲1.8％）と 4四半期ぶりのマイナスを予測する。財貨・
サービスの輸出が前期比 2.0％、財貨・サービスの輸入が同 4.8％となるだろう。代替調達の進展に
伴う原油輸入量の急増を主因として輸入が高い伸
びとなり、輸出の伸びを上回ったことから、外需は
成長率の押し下げ要因となった。 
2026年 7-9月期の地域別輸出数量指数を季節調整
値（当研究所による試算値）でみると、米国向けが
前期比 0.9％（4-6月期：同 2.8％）、EU向けが前期
比▲2.4％（4-6月期：同 0.3％）、アジア向けが前期
比▲0.3％（4-6 月期：同▲0.8％）、うち中国向けが

公司簡介 
志聖成立於 1966 年，目前資本額 16.2 億
新台幣，為 G2C 集團當中的核心成員之一，
以紫外光、烘烤、壓膜塗佈、電漿製程等技
術提供相關壓膜、貼、撕膜、烘烤、電鍍相
關設備。公司初期以 PCB 設備起家，而後
伴隨台灣電子產業升級，切入半導體、組裝、
面板、太陽能等產業，近年陸續在 IC 載板、
高階 HDI、先進封裝、HBM 等高附加價值
市場發光發熱。1-3Q26 營收以產業別劃
分，半導體佔約 45%、先進 PCB 28%、傳
統 PCB 21%、其他電子 4%、FPD 3%。

主要客戶：台積電、日月光、臻鼎、力成、
Amkor、Micron、欣興、勝宏等 
主要競爭對手：EVG、TOK、SUSS、Yield 
Engineering Systems(YES)等

王彥鈞 stanley.wang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：座談會

志聖 (2467 TT) 
能見度高，獲利待驗證

 投資評價與建議 
維持買進，目標價降至 358 元：(1) 3Q26 獲利表現不如預期，主因 PCB 比重拉升
及面對中國在地價格競爭，(2) 能見度高，但隨企業 AI Server 帶動 HDI/HLC PCB 
與 ABF 載板 2H26-2027 年接力放量，短期獲利改善有限。我們預估 2026/ 2027
年 EPS 為 14.01/19.92 元，目標 PE 則由 30x 下修至 18x，反映市場對設備類股評
價的調整，目標價隨之下修到 358 元(18x 2027 EPSF)。

營業利益(百萬) 毛利率

### Page 2

2

志聖以光與熱為核心，發展熱處理平台：志聖成立於 1966 年，目前資本額 16.2 億
新台幣，為 G2C 集團當中的核心成員之一，以紫外光、烘烤、壓膜塗佈、電漿製
程等技術提供相關壓膜、貼、撕膜、烘烤、電鍍相關設備。公司初期以 PCB 設備
起家，而後伴隨台灣電子產業升級，切入半導體、組裝、面板、太陽能等產業，近
年陸續在 IC 載板、高階 HDI、先進封裝、HBM 等高附加價值市場發光發熱。公司
也與均豪、均華、東捷組成 G2C+聯盟，共同實現台灣先進封裝在地化。志聖主要
生產據點位於台灣林口、台中、中國廣州、昆山，並在台南、廣東、泰國、美國鳳
凰城有服務據點。1-3Q26 營收以產業別劃分，半導體佔約 45%、先進 PCB 28%、
傳統 PCB 21%、其他電子 4%、FPD 3%。主要競爭對手包含 EVG、TOK、SUSS、
Yield Engineering Systems(YES)等。客戶包含台積電、日月光、臻鼎、力成、
Amkor、Micron、欣興、勝宏等電子中上游廠商。 
3Q26 獲利不如預期：志聖 3Q26 營收 29.8 億(+7.0%QoQ，+93.9%YoY)，毛利
率 43.3%，稅後淨利 5.5 億(-13.4%QoQ，+162.1%YoY)，EPS 3.51 元。毛利率
表現不如預期主因出貨較多中國 PCB 用低階設備，使產品組合轉差，即半導體設
備/PCB 設備由 1H26 的 46%/46%變動至 1-3Q26 的 45%/49%，營業費用亦隨
獎酬增加及研發專案數的提升而高於預期。 
能見度高，但我們對獲利趨於審慎：管理層預期 2026 年底在手訂單約 100-110
億，較去年同期約 43 億元倍增，其中半導體佔比約 53%以上(以 OSAT 能見度較
高)、高階 PCB 35-37%。值得注意的是，半導體設備交期已由 4-10 個月拉長至 4-
13 個月；PCB 設備交期則約 3-8 個月，主因 OSAT 在廠房未完工前即下單，以及
上游零組件(機械手臂)交期的拉長所致，志聖持續以 G2C+聯盟和日系供應商溝通
並聯合採購，目前風險可控。展望後市，我們預期在晶圓廠先進封裝產能建置外溢
的環境下，志聖將透過挑單生產，降低傳統 PCB 接單比例以維持獲利率穩健成長。 
載板挖槽嵌入製程，2028 年再添動能：隨 AI 晶片尺寸持續放大，台系載板廠正
發展新的生產方式，以降低整片翹曲同時達到微縮線寬/線距的目的。志聖在此製
程中提供背面貼膜，以及 ABF 絕緣膜的壓合與撕膜設備。由於層數倍增將使壓合
與撕膜次數同步增加，加上載具尺寸放大推升單機價值，我們認為這將是志聖 
2028 年後載板設備的重要成長來源。 
財務預估：研究部預估志聖 2026 年營收 111.1 億元(+82.2%YoY)，毛利率 46.3%，
稅後淨利 22.7 億元(+173.0%YoY)，EPS 14.01 元(原估 16.94 元，下修主因本業
獲利表現不如預期及股本膨脹)。展望 2027 年，公司尚未給予明確展望，我們考量
短期 PCB/HLC 急單湧現壓抑毛利率，故下修獲利預估，預期 2027 年營收 148.4
億元(+33.5%YoY)，毛利率 46.9%，稅後淨利 32.2 億元(+42.2%YoY)，EPS 19.92
元(原估 25.15 元，下修主因獲利預估調降及股本膨脹)。

### Page 1

ab 17 November 2026
Global Research
UBS Global I/O Semiconductors
New US Export Controls on Cloud Access;
Assessing the Impact
Summary Thoughts
The US Dept. of Commerce Bureau of Industry and Security (BIS) posted a new rule
addressing remote access to advanced semiconductors. Following discussions with
industry experts (including a former Commerce Dept. official), we believe the rule  is
essentially attempting to accomplish the same thing as the AI Diffusion Rule issued by

the Biden Administration, but never put into effect. We would note that the rule was
"written by, and at the direction of the  Department of Treasury" - atypical for such a
rule, according to our experts. While we can't say for sure that this is in direct response
to the NVDA situation, the timing seems highly coincidental and we highlighted this risk
here and in our estimate cuts yesterday morning. We would make a few key points:
 Demand impact is hard to assess, but could be significant. Current export

control laws have not required a license  for shipments to key countries such as
Malaysia, Thailand, Indonesia, and Singapore (though we note  there is little room
for data centers in Singapore). As a result, we have seen a large increase in data
center construction in Malaysia in particular with recent press reports from the
NYT and Economist  (here and here) suggesting ~2GW of new data centers now
online in a single province in Malaysia with another ~10GW currently under

construction, and  total approved volume up to ~25GW with backing from both US
and China hyperscalers. On this basis, it seems fair to assume  a  run-rate for
shipments into Malaysia alone of a few GW/yr and the NYT article indicated that
about half of this is going to China-domiciled  companies. There is a carve-out in
the new rule for online retailers and social media companies but we would think
that US companies would err on the side of conservatism unless they can be

continuously assured of the end customer. Ergo, it is possible that this new rule
could strip 1-2GW out of current demand, with most of this likely being from
NVDA. At $30B+/GW, this is roughly consistent with our estimate of a 10%
demand impact, though NVDA should still be able to divert these shipments to
other customers to mitigate any near-term impact so we see this as more of a
longer-term issue especially if powered shell permitting gets more restrictive in the

US.
 Odds of MATCH Act passage have gone up, but timing remains a key Q. We
have written in the past about  MATCH  here and here. While the US may not want
to enforce additional export controls on the heels of this new cloud access rule,
our conversations with experts marked a clear shift in tone around the odds of the
MATCH Act passing vs. prior months - with the legislation potentially being
attached to the NDAA in this upcoming Congress. That being said, an expert firmly

believes that the disparity between what ASML, TEL, and other foreign equipment
suppliers currently sell into China vs. US SPE suppliers is not all that significant,
making the rule more of a point of tension with US allies such as the Netherlands
and Japan. Second, the goal of this legislation would be "level the playing field"
using the FDPR (Foreign Direct Product Rule) but there is a question of who would
determine which tools sold by the Dutch and Japanese suppliers are “equivalent”

to those currently restricted for US equipment suppliers. Third, a contemplated
ban on immersion DUV would make supporting trailing-edge fabs and lines in
China quite challenging, with potential spillover effects across the industry, even
outside China. Fourth, it raises the question of what incentives, if any, the Dutch
and Japanese governments would have to comply. Related to this, an expert
indicated that the use of FDPR by the USG could be more upsetting than tariffs

### Page 2

directly impacted by this rule, but in our coverage, we see KLAC being the only one
really indirectly negatively affected by the passage of this rule given its high attach
to litho process steps (and others affected to a lesser extent, though less litho
could in fact ripple to fewer dep/etch tools).
Read Across
We see NVDA as the most directly impacted by this rule, though the entire supply chain,
from memory through SPE, would ultimately be negatively affected by the closure of

such a large demand corridor. That said, we could argue that, over the longer term,
approving  the MATCH Act could benefit memory suppliers outside China by
constraining domestic Chinese capacity additions.

in the economy, a disruption of international trade, technological disruption due to new
inventions, or business model innovation whereby structural changes in the industry alter the
future course of unit sales, ASPs, and revenues.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Kinsus Interconnect Technology (3189.TW)
25 November 2026 Citi Research
2
Kinsus Interconnect Technology
Valuation
Our DCF-based target price for Kinsus is NT$950. Factoring in a 5.0% risk-free rate (YTD avg. 10-year government bond), a 7%
risk premium and beta of 1.38, we calculate a WACC of 14.3%. Our DCF model assumes 1.5% long-term revenue growth and a
31.5% EBIT margin in 2028E with gradual margin improvement going forward. We believe the worst of demand/profitability

headwinds are over given recovery demand and more disciplined supply control by the industry. Our DCF-based target price
equates to 26/15X 2027/28E EPS and 7.8x/5.7x 2027/28E BVPS.
Risks
Key downside risks that could prevent the Kinsus stock from reaching our target price include: 1) weaker smartphone demand.;
2) weaker-than-expected ABF demand momentum or market oversupply; and 3) lower-than-expected contribution from AiP.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

buy recommendation and a Catalyst Watch/STV Downside call corresponds to a sell recommendation. Any stock not assigned
to a Catalyst Watch Upside, Catalyst Watch Downside, STV Upside, or STV Downside call is considered Catalyst Watch/STV
Prepared for Kevin Lu

catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

貿聯-KY (3665 TT) 
併購綜效超預期，獲利動能再加速

永豐觀點 
併購 Interplex、新富生貢獻雙雙超預期，獲利上修循環啟動。 
 投資評價與建議 
重申買進建議：研究部本次上修貿聯財務預估：(1) Interplex 資料通訊業務
(Datacom Business)營收規模大幅超標：10 月首個完整併表月營收約新台幣 21
億元，大幅優於研究部原先預期之約 13 億元，研究部同步上修其 2026~2028 年
營收與獲利貢獻預估。(2) 新富生月營收規模自年初以來已成長逾一倍，憑藉資料
中心高階光纖跳線的製造與品質優勢，持續拓展新客戶，研究部預估其 2027 年營
收可持續倍增。(3) 產品組合轉佳、營運槓桿持續發揮：3Q26 毛利率季增 1.8 個
百點至 32.53%，且營業費用率降至約 12%，帶動營益率創 20.78%新高。綜合預
估貿聯 2026-2028 年集團營收分別為 1,074、1,659、2,161 億元(+51%、+55%、
+30%YoY)；稅後淨利 150、267、356 元(+66%、+79%、+33%YoY)，完全稀
釋後 EPS 分別為 75.25、134.07 及 178.24 元。考量 AI 供應鏈遇短期逆風，影響
評價表現，研究部將本益比調整為 18 倍，維持買進建議，目標價 2,810 元(2027
年及 2028 年之平均預估 EPS 156.16*18XPER)。

ESG 評析 
貿聯企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B 等與 A 等。

### Page 2

貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：貿聯-KY 是全球領先的連接器公司之一：
貿聯-KY 總部設於加州 Fremont，3Q26 產品比重為資訊科技與數據傳輸 48％、
工業用 31％、車用 8％、電器 12％；若以事業別區分，HPC 占 46％、半導體設
備(SPE)占 13％、其他事業占 41％。貿聯主要核心能力，在於高技術及客製化開
發零組件製造，產品開發以多角化及差異化策略，2026/1 完成收購新富生光電
(XFS)，2026/9/23 完成收購 Interplex 集團資料通訊業務(Interplex Datacom)，
產品線由電源與數據連接延伸至光纖跳線、機殼及機櫃等精密機構件，朝 AI 機櫃
次系統解決方案供應商發展。 
3Q26 財報公布，優於市場預期，營收與獲利同創新高，營益率首度突破 20%：
3Q26 營收 278.75 億元(+19.7% QoQ、+51.4% YoY)，毛利率 32.53%，季增 1.8
個百分點；營業利益率 20.78%，季增 3.6 個百分點、年增 1.2 個百分點，為公司
首度突破 20%，獲利表現優於市場預期。成長主要來自 HPC，3Q26 HPC 營收季
增 30%、年增 61%，營收比重由 2Q26 的 42%回升至 46%，其中電源產品為成
長主力，帶動產品組合改善；半導體設備營收亦季增 11%、年增 70%，各事業全
面成長。費用方面，營收規模擴大但營業費用未等比例增加，營業費用率降至約
12%的歷史低點，顯示獲利率改善來自規模效益與費用效率，而非一次性因素，具
備延續性。稅後淨利 42.93 億元(+44.0% QoQ、+63.3% YoY)，稀釋後 EPS 21.51
元，營收、淨利與 EPS 皆創歷史新高；前三季累計 EPS 48.04 元，已超越 2025 全
年之 46.07 元。

### Page 3

3

研究部上修 2026~2028 年營收獲利預估，原因有三： 
1. Interplex 資料通訊業務(Datacom Business)營收提前兩年達標：Interplex
的資料通訊業務(Datacom Business)自 9/23 起併表，3Q26 僅貢獻 7 天營
收；10 月為首個完整併表月，帶動貿聯 10 月營收達 118.46 億元，月增 26.9%、
年增 80.0%，其中 Interplex 貢獻 6,700 萬美元(約新台幣 21 億元)。對照公司
宣布併購時揭露之年營收 3.92 億美元(月平均約 3,300 萬美元)，Interplex 月
營收已倍增，大幅優於市場預期。研究部看好併購綜效將逐步顯現：Interplex
主要供應 AI 伺服器機殼、機櫃等精密機構件，研究部認為其營收較併購時大
幅成長，主因 AI 機櫃出貨量較年初明顯放大，且併入貿聯後，Interplex 可運
用集團的資金、客戶關係與製造資源擴大接單，雙方產品整合後可提供涵蓋配
電、數據連接、機構件與液冷的機櫃次系統方案，單一 AI 機櫃之可供應產值
明顯擴大。研究部本次上修 2027、2028 年 Interplex Datacom Business 為
貿聯帶來的營收預估，分別為 268、308 億元(原預估 183、250 億元)。 
2. 新富生光通訊規模快速放大，營收占比持續提升：新富生(XFS) 專精於光纖互
連產品的設計與製造，主要產品包括單芯／雙芯跳線、光纖尾纖、多芯光纖以
及 MTP/MPO 高密度連接器，月營收規模自 2026 年初以來成長逾一倍，獲
利能力同步改善，展現規模化高精密光學互連業務的效益。資料中心對光纖組
件的品質與一致性要求遠高於一般應用，新富生在高階光纖跳線的製造經驗與
品質優勢，加上貿聯集團的客戶網絡與全球產能布局，有助其持續拓展新客戶。
隨 AI 資料中心機櫃間光纖連接需求成長，研究部預估新富生 2027 年營收可
望再翻倍增長，且其獲利率改善亦有助提升貿聯整體獲利結構。 
3. 3Q26 營益率創新高，營運槓桿持續發揮：3Q26 營業費用率約 12%，較 1H26
的 13.7%明顯下降，顯示貿聯在承接更大規模、更複雜的客戶專案時，製造、
工程與行政職能的效率同步提升，費用無須等比例增加。4Q26 起 Interplex
完整併表，營收規模再擴大，研究部預期營益率改善幅度可望大於毛利率，稅
後淨利成長幅度將高於營收。 
HPC 電源成長最強，新平台升級為下一波動能：3Q26 HPC 營收年增 61%、季增
30%，前三季累計年增 77%。隨運算功耗提升，電源產品切入規格更高的新一代
平台；數據連接方面，貿聯同時布局銅纜與光纖，因應不同傳輸距離與頻寬需求。
貿聯於 10 月 OCP Global Summit 2026 展出適用於 OCP 及 NVIDIA MGX 機櫃
之 800V/400VDC 高功率電源方案，以及 1.6T 液冷交換器 OSFP 連接器、機櫃管
理控制與漏液偵測系統，產品範圍由電源線材延伸至配電、液冷與監控。研究部認
為，隨 Vera Rubin 平台放量及 HVDC 架構導入，單一機櫃的電源產品規格與產值
將持續提升，為 2027 年 HPC 成長主軸。 
次世代光通訊布局：貿聯 2026 年先後於 OFC 展示 Optical Shuffle、於 Computex
展示 CPO 交換器托盤方案；9 月於 ECOC 2026 展示與 ams OSRAM 合作之
microVCSEL 光學互連方案，採用 850nm 二維可定址 microVCSEL 陣列，以多通
道、低速率(wide-and-slow)架構降低功耗與延遲，貿聯負責光纖陣列組裝、光學
封裝、光學測試與量產製造；10 月於 OCP Global Summit 2026 展出 CPO、
800G/1.6T 及 XPO 光收發模組、光纖陣列(FAU)與多芯光纖連接等方案。公司表
示產業未來可能並行採用多種光學架構，貿聯以製造與整合能力參與各技術路線，
不需押注單一方案。 
半導體設備成長幅度為產業兩倍以上：3Q26 半導體設備(SPE)營收年增 70%、季
增 11%，營收占比由 3Q25 的 11%提升至 13%，前三季累計年增 48%。根據國
際半導體產業協會（SEMI）於 2026 年 7 月公布之預測，全球前段晶圓製造設備

### Page 4

4

(WFE)市場 2026 年預估年增 23.1%至 1,439 億美元，貿聯 SPE 成長幅度約為產業
的兩倍；自 2023 年以來貿聯之 SPE 業務成長即大幅超越整體 WFE 市場，超額成
長主因貿聯在客戶端的角色由供應線材零組件，擴大至高階組裝、模組與次系統製
造、測試及系統整合，應用以薄膜沉積與蝕刻設備為主，微影與檢測設備為輔；隨
設備製程日益複雜，客戶傾向將更多製造工作委外，以集中資源於核心技術，貿聯
單一設備平台之內含價值隨之提升。SEMI 預估 WFE 市場 2027 年及 2028 年將分
別成長 21.8%及 14.1%，2028 年規模可望突破 2,000 億美元，主要由先進邏輯、
HBM 等先進記憶體投資帶動；公司亦表示，許多客戶對設備投資的強勁預期延伸
至 2030 年以後，且客戶正主動協助貿聯擴充產能，以支援其自身長期成長。貿聯
在 SPE 領域的投資以擴充製造產能為主，Interplex 的精密機械加工與次系統整合
能力亦可與 SPE 業務互補，為 HPC 以外的第二成長引擎。 
隨伺服器架構升級驅動產值倍增，2027~2028 年獲利成長路徑清晰，重申買進： 
研究部認為，貿聯本次財報的關鍵不只是單季獲利優於預期，而是兩項併購同時優
於預期，帶動營收基期結構性墊高：10 月營收 118.46 億元，較 3Q26 月平均約
93 億元高出約 27%。在 AI 機櫃同時需要電源、數據傳輸(銅纜與光纖)與精密機構
件的趨勢下，貿聯透過 Interplex 與新富生補齊產品版圖，單一機櫃可供應產值持
續擴大；而未來一年內，Interplex 完整併表(4Q26)、新富生營收比重提升(2027)、
Vera Rubin 平台與 HVDC 電 源 放 量 (2027~2028) 及 次 世 代 光 互 連 產 品
(2H27~2028)等成長催化劑將陸續到位，獲利上修動能明確。 
評價方面，短期因市場擔憂 GPU 產品推出節奏可能放緩，台灣 AI 供應鏈評價短
期受到壓抑。研究部認為，CSP 資本支出與現行平台訂單短期不受影響，且貿聯的
客戶與產品布局相對分散：公司採取平台中立策略，產品同時供應 GPU 與 ASIC
架構之 AI 機櫃，並服務多家雲端服務業者；銅纜與光纖、電源與機構件等產品線
亦不依賴單一技術路線；此外，半導體設備與其他事業合計仍占營收逾五成，營運
受單一平台影響的程度低於純 GPU 供應鏈。惟考量主要客戶經營層異動可能影響
新平台推出節奏，研究部於評價上給予折價，本益比由前次之 25 倍調整為 18 倍。
搭配本次上修後之 EPS，維持買進建議，目標價 2,810 元(2027 年及 2028 年之平
均預估 EPS 156.16*18XPER)。

Document 257: AI is boosting business creations both in the US and Europe (2026-11-04)

### Page 1

4 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AI IS BOOSTING BUSINESS CREATIONS BOTH IN THE US AND EUROPE
Stéphane Colliac
The significant increase in business creations since mid-2025 in the United States, France and Germany – and, to a lesser extent, in the United
Kingdom – is continuing. Across the board, this trend is being driven by the information and communication sector and mostly by AI. It is
spreading to other business services, while developments in consumer services, industry and construction are more mixed. However, in both

the United States and France, the trend is broadening, suggesting that AI is facilitating business creation across all sectors.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 4 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A noticeable boom in the United States, France, and Germany, but not quite as pronounced in the United Kingdom
The four economies for which we have comparable data (the United States, France, Germany and the United Kingdom) have all
seen, to varying degrees, an increase in the number of new business creations. While the timing varies across these countries,

most of them are enjoying strong momentum. In the United States, there has been a noticeable acceleration since the second
quarter of 2025, which has continued to strengthen. Germany has followed a similar trajectory, moving from slight declines at
the start of 2024 to sharp increases. France is experiencing the same trend but with some specific features, as momentum has
picked up significantly after months of slowdown (due to the uncertainty following the dissolution of the National Assembly) and is

accelerating across most sectors in 2026. In the United Kingdom, the trend is less pronounced and the increase may not last.
Business services, and AI in particular, are driving this momentum
In most of these four countries, business creations are rising significantly in the information and communication sector, which is
directly linked to AI. In the United States, Germany and France, there has been a pronounced trend which is gaining momentum.
In the United Kingdom, the AI sector is also seeing an increase, but it is significantly less pronounced. However, this momentum is

not limited to this sector. It is spreading to many services sectors, including finance, real estate and business services. Growth has
been sustained since mid-2025 in the United States and Germany, and since the third quarter of 2025 in France.
The picture for consumer services is more mixed. In the United States, trade, accommodation and catering have experienced major
increases in creations since mid-2025. In France, these sectors are in positive territory, particularly trade, where the increase in

business creations has recently accelerated. In Germany, these same sectors fell into negative territory in 2026 following a rather
mixed year in 2025.
The situation is even more mixed for manufacturing and construction. In the United States, these sectors are also part of the
overall positive momentum in the third quarter of 2026. This shows that AI does not only benefit sectors closely linked to it
(notably the digital technology sector) but that it makes it easier to start a business across the entire economy (by simplifying

administrative procedures in particular). In France, the upturn in business creations in the construction sector suggests that this
same trend is at play, even as activity in the sector continues to contract.
Therefore, the trend is more widespread in the United States and is becoming increasingly prevalent in France. It is driven by
specific sectors of the services industry in Germany, while it is significantly more subdued in the United Kingdom.

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

Document 262: AI巨头被曝藏着密码学“核弹”，量子计算大牛Scott Aaronson博客刷屏

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI巨头被曝藏着密码学“核弹”，量子计算大牛Scott Aaronson博客刷屏.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-11-27
Pages: 9
Status: ok

### Page 1

他在博客中透露，他⼏乎可以确信AI实验室已取得尚未公开的重⼤密码学突破，引发外界
对RSA和椭圆曲线加密安全性的担忧。与此同时，⾕歌AI已在整数分解等领域取得进展，
英国央⾏也警⽰前沿AI带来的⽹络安全⻛险，敦促⾦融机构加快密码技术升级。
摘要：
AI可能已经找到了破解现有密码系统的⽅法，只是还没公布。
不er，OpenAI和A社到底还藏了多少东西？？
前脚两家才双双宣布证明了P vs NP的代数版本VP≠VNP。
后脚，理论计算机科学界⼤佬Scott Aaronson就爆出了⼀个更惊⼈的消息：
（标题也是相当直⽩了，HOLY SHITx4）
u1s1，要是别⼈说这话我可能还有所怀疑，但Scott就不⼀样了。
这位是得克萨斯⼤学奥斯汀分校计算机科学教授，量⼦计算和计算复杂性理论领域的知名⼤
佬。
⽽且，他还有⼀重相当微妙的⾝份——OpenAI前研究员，曾在OpenAI专⻔研究AI安全问题。
专业对⼝，⼜在AI⼤⼚待过，能⽐咱们提前吃到点⽠，倒也不奇怪。（doge）
也难怪Scott的爆料⼀出，数学圈、AI圈、密码学圈的⼈瞬间坐不住了。。。

我从多个可靠消息源获悉，⼏乎可以确信，AI实验室⼿⾥还握着⼀枚甚⾄多枚密码学「重磅炸
弹」。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP会员 ⼤师课 ⻅闻历 搜索

### Page 2

⼤家猜来猜去，最终将⽬光锁定在了⼀个关键问题上：
⽀撑RSA加密体系的⼤整数分解难题，该不会已经被AI拿下了吧？？
要知道这事⼉⼀旦成真，影响的可就不只是数学界了。
咱们平时⽹购付款、登录⽹站、传输机密⽂件，背后都有密码技术在保驾护航，⽽RSA正是其
中⼴泛使⽤的公钥密码算法之⼀。
兹事体⼤，连《Nature》都闻着味⼉来了，专⻔发⽂追问：
AI已经破解互联⽹加密了吗？
OpenAI和A社，⼿⾥可能还有⼤招
这事⼉还得从Scott的⼀顿晚饭说起。
就在OpenAI和A社双双宣布证明VP≠VNP的当天，他正和⼏位朋友、同事⼀起聚餐。
结果⼀顿饭的⼯夫，OpenAI⼜接连放出了矩阵乘法、快速傅⾥叶变换相关的数学研究成果。
这速度，直接给⼀桌⼦研究理论计算机科学的⼈整不会了。
⼤家⼀边吃饭，⼀边拿着⼿机和电脑，疯狂刷新OpenAI和A社的GitHub仓库。
毕竟现在这两家连新闻稿都懒得发了，谁知道下⼀次刷新，⼜有哪个困扰⼈类⼏⼗年的数学难
题被解决了。。。

### Page 3

⽽且疯狂刷新之外，曾在OpenAI待过的Scott不免担⼼——
放出来的成果都已经这么惊⼈了，那些还没放出来的呢？
事实上，早在上⼀篇博客的评论区，就有⽹友向他打听过：
OpenAI或者Anthropic，是不是已经找到了整数分解的多项式时间算法（Factoring in P）？
彼时，Scott就透露，⾃⼰已经听到了不少传闻，称OpenAI和A社⾄少有⼀家已经找到了这样的
算法。
不过，眼看这事⼉越传越⼴，Scott后来⼜给这条回复加上了删除线，提醒⼤家不要把传闻当成
实锤。
⽽到了最新这篇博客⾥，Scott⼲脆把⾃⼰能够确认到什么程度，给说清楚了：
I’ve now heard many, many rumors that one or both labs are sitting on Factoring in P.
我⼏乎可以确信，AI实验室⼿⾥还握着尚未公开的重⼤密码学突破。⾄于具体是不是整数分解，
⽬前还不好说。

### Page 4

不er，为啥⼤家都盯上了整数分解啊？
咱们来做⼀道数学题。
17×23等于多少？
391，虽然已经过了智商巅峰期，但花点时间⼝算⼀下咱还是能搞定。
但如果反过来，只告诉你391，让你找出它是哪两个质数相乘得到的呢？
显然就要多费点⼯夫了。
再把这两个质数换成各有⼏百位的数字，正着乘，对计算机来说依旧轻轻松松，反过来分解难
度却会急剧飙升。
RSA加密算法的重要安全基础，恰恰就是这种计算难度上的不对称。
过去⼏⼗年，数学家们⼀直在尝试寻找更⾼效的整数分解算法。
如果AI真的找到了⾜够实⽤的多项式时间算法，那么RSA的安全基础也就被动摇了。
⽽且，还真别说，已经有AI开始在这个⽅向上交作业了。
就在11⽉25⽇，⾕歌⼀⼝⽓公开了6篇由AI系统Cogentic⽣成的数学论⽂。

### Page 5

仔细⼀看，其中有两篇研究的正是整数分解，还有⼀篇涉及密码学中的另⼀个重要数学难题
——离散对数。
虽然⾕歌⽬前公开的整数分解算法还没有达到多项式时间，但⾄少从这些论⽂来看，AI已经开始
在密码学所依赖的数学难题上取得进展了。
然后，问题就来了——
⾕歌已经在这个⽅向上交了作业，那最近动不动就给数学界整出⼀场「地震」的OA两家呢？
再结合Scott的爆料……
嘶，不敢细想。。。
甚⾄有reddit⽹友已经发现了⼀个耐⼈寻味的细节：
就，真的很不对劲。
以前抢着发论⽂，现在可能不敢发了？
反正坐不住，那不如反向「试探」OpenAI⼀把？？
此前OpenAI⼀⼝⽓公布了涉及722道数学问题的研究成果，⼏乎涵盖数学的各个领域，唯独不
⻅密码学的踪影。

### Page 6

就在今天，X上有位叫Cassie Heart的⽹友爆料，⾃⼰在研究ECC（椭圆曲线密码学）时，遇到
了⼀件怪事。
同样的研究思路，之前GPT Astra 6还愿意陪⾃⼰往下研究，换成Astra 6.2 ，诶，直接不让聊
了。
这位⽹友当即就警觉起来：
要知道，ECC和前⾯提到的RSA⼀样，也是当今主流的公钥密码技术，⼴泛应⽤于HTTPS安全
通信、数字签名等场景。
为了防⽌哪天OpenAI突然甩出⼀篇论⽂，把功劳全揽⾛了，这位⽹友索性把⾃⼰研究思路对应
的SHA-256哈希值给Po到了⽹上，提前留个证。
当然，光凭模型拒绝继续讨论，还不能说明OpenAI真的取得了突破。
但这事⼉想想也挺微妙的。
前⼏天，OA两家为了争夺VP≠VNP的⾸发权，恨不得把做出证明的时间精确到⼏点⼏分。
可如果真有⼀家率先攻破了RSA或者ECC……
这第⼀，还敢抢吗？
毕竟，按照斯坦福⼤学知名密码学家Dan Boneh的说法，⼀旦AI找到了破解主流公钥密码系统
的新⽅法，整个互联⽹都可能受到波及。
更关键的是，这事⼉甚⾄都不⽤等到P vs NP问题被解决。

AI Weekly在报道此事时就特意指出，RSA和ECC所依赖的数学难题，⾄今都没有被证明是NP
完全问题。
就是说，AI甚⾄都不⽤拿下P vs NP这个世纪难题，就有可能攻破现有的密码系统。
OpenAI该不会已经快把ECC给破了吧？？

### Page 7

想到这⼉，再回头看看最近数学圈的画⻛，就有点魔幻了。
随着AI接连拿下数学难题，⽹上也掀起了⼀股给AI「找茬」的热潮。
有⼈质疑证明的正确性，有⼈质疑AI究竟有没有原创能⼒，还有⼈⼲脆让AI去反驳AI给出的证
明。
⼀时间，数学证明和针对证明的「反驳」满天⻜。
对此，Scott也在博客⾥引⽤了⾃⼰⼀位朋友的评论，表达了态度：
Anyway，这事⼉短时间内也很难吵出个结果。
但考虑到AI可能带来的⻛险，与密码学深度绑定的⾦融圈已经开始⾏动了。
就在当地时间11⽉26⽇，英国央⾏发布了最新⼀期⾦融稳定报告。
报告专⻔提到了前沿AI可能带来的⽹络安全⻛险，同时还在催促⾦融机构加快向抗量⼦密码技术
迁移。
经过多年来那些声称千禧年难题已被解决的荒谬「解答」之后，现在当我们有了真正的解答时，
却⼜出现了荒谬的「反驳」，声称千禧年难题并未被解决。从某种意义上说，这简直是个绝妙的
讽刺。

### Page 8

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
64 收藏 分享到:
写评论
说起来，过去这么多年，⼈类⼀直在为量⼦计算机可能带来的密码学威胁做准备。
新的密码算法都研究出来了，各国也陆陆续续开始部署了。
谁承想呢？
量⼦计算机还没等来，隔壁天天做数学题的AI倒是可能先来抢活了。。。
⽽且，抗量⼦密码算法也有⾃⼰的数学安全假设，能防住量⼦计算机的已知攻击算法，可不意
味着就能防住AI找到的全新解法。
最扎⼼的是，照现在这个势头，AI在数学上取得⼀次重⼤突破，可能就是Scott吃顿晚饭的⼯
夫。
但全球银⾏、企业、政府机构要想把⾃家的密码系统全换⼀遍……
那可不是多吃⼏顿饭就能解决的事⼉了。
One More Thing
在⻅证近期⼀系列AI数学突破后，Scott第⼀次真切地感受到，「P≠NP的证明可能已经不远了」。
他甚⾄感慨，过去这⼀周发⽣的事情，和⾃⼰想象中AI奇点到来的样⼦⾮常相似。

只希望这场奇点，仅仅局限在他们那个研究数学难题的⼩⼩世界⾥。
但现在看来，好像已经有点难了。。。

133 收藏 分享到:
美国AI编程初创公司Cognition CEO Scott Wu警告，未来6⾄12个⽉全球可能出现⼤量重
⼤数据泄露，AI智能体从单体执⾏转向“蜂群”协同，正⼤幅提升⽹络攻击的速度与规模。他
认为，企业和政府亟需加强AI防御部署，同时完善智能体监测、权限管理与安全护栏。
摘要：
AI正在改变⽹络攻击的规模与速度，全球企业和政府机构可能⾯临新⼀轮⽹络安全危机。
美国AI编程初创公司Cognition⾸席执⾏官Scott Wu警告，按当前趋势，未来6⾄12个⽉全球可
能出现“⼤量重⼤数据泄露”，财富50强、财富100强企业及政府机构均处于⾼⻛险之下。他在播
客节⽬《Invest Like the Best》中称，这⼀趋势“⼏乎不可避免”。
Wu认为，真正的分⽔岭在于防御能⼒能否跟上攻击技术的演进：企业和政府是否能够⼤规模引

⼊AI加强防御，还是任由⻛险在未来数年内失控。
11⽉以来，多起数据泄露事件接连发⽣，导致航空公司停运、数字银⾏平台服务中断，也让外
界对AI助⻓⽹络攻击的担忧升温。尽管尚⽆证据表明这些事件与AI有关，但AI带来的安全⻛险正
受到越来越多关注。
从单体智能体到“蜂群”：攻击模式正在改变
Wu将⻛险上升归因于AI攻击形态的变化：过去由单个AI智能体执⾏任务，如今则演变为多个智
能体协同运作的“蜂群”，能够并⾏尝试⼤量攻击路径，⼤幅提升攻击速度和规模。他认为，这是
技术发展的⼀个临界点。
他提及今年7⽉的⼀起事件，称OpenAI模型⾃⾏攻破了初创公司Hugging Face。这类事件令科
技圈感到不安。Wu指出，过去攻击者受限于时间、精⼒和技术能⼒，难以穷尽所有攻击路径，
如今AI蜂群正在改变这⼀局⾯。
⾯对新的攻击模式，Wu强调，企业必须具备更强的监测和控制能⼒，包括及时发现异常、掌握
AI智能体的运⾏情况，以及设置合理的权限限制和安全护栏。随着企业加速部署AI智能体，如何
监控和约束这些系统，正成为⽹络安全防御的重要环节。

两党智库战略与国际研究中⼼（CSIS）近期也指出，2027年的美国国会很可能需要应对危险数
据泄露问题，此类事件近期已严重影响美国⾏政部⻔的正常运转。

Document 268: An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-27)

Source PDF: all report pdf/An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-27).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
An RBA peak of greater conviction
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
08:56 November 13 2026 Share
We already believed the RBA cash rate was likely at a peak, even if RBA officials were less
convinced. A deeper housing downturn and early signs of cracks in the consumer sector
point to an even softer 2027. Fading inflation risks from oil also boost our conviction on
rates being at a peak.

### Page 2

It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking the cash rate after the November
increase. Since then, our conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we will publish
the November Market Outlook with revised forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing
market have all been revised down in 2027. Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for calendar 2027 more decisively inside the

RBA’s 2–3% target range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment rate forecast has
been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash
rate from here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.
A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The housing market has undershot our
forecasts over the past couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been coming in
under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan inquiries have taken another leg down in October after

the September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior months, with the additional impact
of the November rate hike still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer. Consumer income and spending have
so far been quite resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest rates. But our Westpac
Card Tracker is starting to show that discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite weak. We would not over-emphasise

this point, but it does imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB) meeting, business survey data
has shown weaker conditions and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show further
evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-labour cost shock that is not being fully
passed through to consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil and fuel prices are already materially

lower than the track assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-fourth lower than
on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of fuel costs to other prices, as well as
easing consumer inflation perceptions and expectations.
In the media conference following the November meeting, Governor Bullock flagged that it was a

good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the market read this as a
signal that the MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to downside risks than the internal
members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is looking even less likely given signs
in the data and surveys. The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and senior

officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though, a rate hike in 2027 would require
either a material new inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would constitute a policy
error on the part of the RBA (something that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the
talents and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with the dissenting voters in the MPB

that the downside risks need more attention. Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to February, we think there will be more
evidence that the domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation outlook with it.

the labour market while showing significant concern over
the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the

at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI

Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also
kept the focus on inflation risks and stated or implied the FOMC

needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s
updates, pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January
meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by September. We believe
this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core
inflation ex shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-
month annualised basis and the labour market is balanced; but
it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate,

US households are under pressure from the cost of living, and
their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing
costs. Highlighting this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in
October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and
2010. Our November Market Outlook provide a full update on
our expectations for the US and the world.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index

fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest reading since
late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of
severe economic stress. Renewed cost-of-living and interest
rate pressures were front and centre following another lift
in inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the
RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage holders are feeling
the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group
falling to a record low. Consumers have become noticeably

more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the
relevant sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its
long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business
survey showed confidence slipped further to –15 in October as
conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic.
Of concern for the outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also
the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow
materially faster than selling prices, further compressing
already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter
financial conditions can jolt activity. The value of new dwelling
finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly
decline in the series’ history, taking approvals 25% lower year-
to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined

with the housing tax changes announced in May. Owner-
occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home
buyers proving particularly sensitive to the deterioration in
affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum
across the domestic economy, consistent with Westpac’s
expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside
activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our

expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the consensus
view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the
result, but core inflation was also marginally stronger than
forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the
past four months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look
to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost inflation through.
Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace,
gaining 0.3% in October.

The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated
by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor Waller prior to the
release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and
Elliot Clarke, Head of International Economics

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Post election, where are we left?
This week markets and the economy needed to digest
the General Election results, as well as data on how the
economy is tracking through the final months of the year. A
centre-right coalition looks more likely to ultimately prevail,

although it may be a while before we know for sure and
longer again before a stable government emerges. Watch
this space as paths to victory for the centre-left remain.
Economic data are consistent with a still-strong external
sector but a weaker domestically-generated growth
impulse. Our upcoming Economic Overview next week will
examine these trends in more depth.
The real star of the show this week was the outcome of the
2026 General Election, which delivered an election night lead

to the centre-right coalition of the National, ACT and NZ First
parties. The preliminary result was a 67-57 seat margin in an
enlarged 131 seat parliament. Hence the centre-right won on
election night with a slender one-seat margin, given that 66
seats are required to form a majority. Financial markets and
the business sector generally cheered this outcome given their
preference for a centre-right government. However, the margins
are tight and there is the potential for the result to switch around

when the final vote count becomes available on November 27.
National leads Labour by narrow margins in two electorates
(Wairarapa and Hamilton East) and could lose those seats
when all is said and done. Also, special votes tend to swing
more to the left than the right historically and hence could
boost the centre-left bloc. The star performer of the election
was the Opportunity Party which debuted in parliament with
7 seats. The centre-left would require their support to get

over the line in all scenarios, whereas the centre-right might or
might not need them depending on how the special votes and
final electorate results play out. There seems a clearer path to
victory for the centre-right – especially if they can come to an
agreement with Opportunity – but clients should be wary of
complacency (which we note may be a risk).
A further complication is that the election left the incumbent
National Party in a much-weakened position as their coalition

partners picked up seats at their expense, and because their
much-reduced share of the party vote (29.2%) meant that
no National Party list MPs made it into parliament. Several
seasoned and senior government ministers needed a list seat
to make it into the new government including Finance Minister
Nicola Willis. Even if the final results confirm a centre-right
coalition win, there will be fierce debate within the coalition
on which party staffs key ministerial roles. ACT party leader

David Seymour is making a strong play for Finance Minister,
which Prime Minister Christopher Luxon is fiercely resisting.
There is the potential for tough and protracted coalition
negotiations to play out well into the Christmas period.
Indeed, I spoke on a post-election panel with an ex-National
Party senior minister this week who expressed the view that
it might not be until Waitangi Day (February 6) before a new
government is formed. Should anything like this play out then

the implications for economic and market uncertainty could
be significant. The initial relief rally in the New Zealand dollar
might prove premature in that scenario.
For interest rate markets, the main conclusion drawn is that as
a centre-left government seems a lower probability prospect,
then so also is the likelihood of a return to a ‘dual mandate’
for the RBNZ. Chief Economist Paul Conway made remarks
this week suggesting ongoing concern with inflation risks,

suggesting he is still in the hike camp at this stage. This has
seen markets firm up short-term OCR expectations such that a
25bp OCR increase in December is now seen as high probability
(85-90% currently). Bond markets cheered the prospect of
potentially tighter fiscal policy that could come with a centre-
right government. The spread between the 10-year government
bond and swap spread narrowed 4 basis points over the week
and the 10-year government bond yield differential to the US

fell to -49 basis points – the most negative since 2019.
Economic data showed further evidence of the two-speed
economy that is showing above-trend growth yet at the same
time feels quite unsatisfying to most voters. The low exchange
rate, generally robust global demand and tight supply
conditions in soft commodity markets have been delivering
consistently strong export returns for a couple of years now.
This week we saw another very strong GDT Pulse auction that

propelled whole milk powder futures higher – up some 20-25%
on year ago levels in USD terms. Significant upside risk for our
2026/27 Farmgate Milk Price forecast of $9.75/kgMS now exists,
which we will be delving into more deeply in our Economic
Overview to be released on Wednesday. The manufacturing
sector appears to remain in a solid state, with the manufacturing
PMI slipping a touch in October to 54.9 from 56.3 in September.
Short-term visitor arrivals have resumed their prior strong

uptrend, growing almost 3% for a second consecutive month
in September and almost 10% higher than a year earlier.
While arrivals from key European markets remain well below
year-earlier levels – likely reflecting the disruption to travel
through key Middle Eastern hubs along with higher jet fuel
prices – this has been more than offset by a very sharp lift in
arrivals from China. Indeed, Chinese arrivals in September
were almost two-thirds higher than the same month last year.

Michael Gordon
Senior Economist

5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
And in the September quarter, arrivals from China surpassed
their pre-pandemic level for the first time. The number of
Australian citizens visiting New Zealand grew just under 6%yr
in September, but growth momentum from New Zealand’s
number one source market (a 30% share) does appear to have

slowed somewhat in recent months.
Less positive trends were evident in the more domestically
orientated areas of the economy. The most concrete indicator
of the NZ economy – concrete poured – dropped back a
touch in the September quarter, while consumer spending
in fell 0.7% in October. We have concerns on the quality
of the consumer spending data given that bank-sourced
data indicates a rise in consumer spending in October. But
nonetheless it’s probably reasonable to infer that higher fuel

costs in October crimped household budgets.
Housing market data for October were also week. House sales
fell and are down almost 22% y/y, while house prices fell in
October for the fifth consecutive month to be down almost
2% y/y. The weak performance of the housing market is likely
another factor weighing on consumer spending and residential
investment. That’s another topic we will be taking a look at in
next week’s Economic Overview.
Net migration flows for September confirm still modest

population growth. Arrivals of non-New Zealand citizens
continues to drift higher while departures declined a touch.
The net outflow of New Zealanders remains relatively high but
is declining, as signs of firmer employment growth emerge in
New Zealand at the same time as the Australian labour market
shows signs of weakening.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Employment Change (000s)
Nov 19, Last: –39.3, Westpac f/c: 30
Market f/c: 27.5, Range: 9.9 to 42
Employment fell by 39.3k in September, well below expectations
for a 20k lift. Some of this weakness may reflect an unwind of
a possible residual seasonality stemming from recent changes

to the survey design, which saw August come in stronger.
Under the monthly noise, the labour market is loosening
gradually as jobs growth runs slower than population growth.
While some leading indicators point to near-term stability
– such as job ads – business surveys and detail within the
employment data point to some downside risk.
For October, we have pencilled in a lift in employment of +30k,
which would see employment growth hold steady on a three-

month average basis.
AUS: Nov Westpac Housing Sentiment (index)
Nov 17, Last: 85.3
The Westpac Housing Sentiment Index that appears in our
regular Housing Pulse reports will now be published on a
monthly basis a week after Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Sentiment is released. The Index is a composite
based on housing-related sentiment measures from the survey
and has proven to be an extremely useful predictor of turning
points in housing markets, both nationally and across states.

At 85.3, the Oct monthly Index read was holding near historic
lows, well below the long run avg of 100. The Nov update will
capture reactions to the RBA’s latest interest rate rise and the
deepening and broadening housing downturn. The release
will also include results from two new questions added to
the regular survey on consumers’ property buying and selling
intentions over the next twelve months.
AUS: Q3 Wage Price Index (%qtr)
Nov 18, Last: 0.8, Westpac f/c: 1.0

Market f/c: 1.0, Range: 0.9 to 1.1
The Wage Price Index (WPI) rose 0.8% in Q2, leaving annual
growth at 3.2%. Private sector wages continued to decelerate,
in line with a softer labour market, while public sector wages
provided a bit of an offset.
The Fair Work Commission's (FWC) 2026 Annual Wage Review
handed down a larger-than-expected 4.75% increase in award
wages. This will have a noticeable impact on the Q3 WPI, as the
wage increases stemming from the review are typically passed

on during the September quarter.
We have pencilled in a rise of 1.0% for Q3, lifting the annual
pace to 3.4%yr. Detail on wages growth by bargaining stream
will be closely watched for evidence of broader diffusion
beyond arrangements directly linked to the FWC's decision.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUS: Oct Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Nov 19, Last: 4.7, Westpac f/c: 4.7
Market f/c: 4.7, Range: 4.6 to 4.8
Once again, the surprise in employment coincided with a surprise
in labour force participation, which fell 0.2ppts to 66.8% from
its near cycle high of 67.0% in August. This translated to a 26.2k

decline in the size of the labour force, which was less than the
drop in employment. As a result, the unemployment rate ticked
slightly higher in September, from 4.65% to 4.72% (although it
was steady at 4.7% to one decimal place), a new cycle high.
For October, we have the participation rate steady at 66.8%,
which together with the forecast rise in employment, would
see the unemployment rate also hold steady at 4.7%. Early
signs of rising retrenchments are a key signal to watch over the

coming months, especially if new hiring across other areas of
the economy starts to dry up.
NZ: Oct Selected Consumer Prices
Nov 17
Stats NZ’s monthly Selected Prices provides an update on
around half of the full CPI, including many of the more volatile
items. In terms of the big groups, we’re expecting a 0.1%
rise in overall food prices, with fresh produce prices typically
soft at this time of year. We also expect continued softness
in housing rents. However, the key focus will be on transport

costs. Petrol prices are expected to be up 11%. And we’ll be
watching to see if high global fuel prices are contributing to
further increases in airfares after recent large increases. There
is related uncertainty around holiday accommodation costs
(which drive much of the month-to-month volatility in overall
prices).
NZ: Q4 RBNZ Survey of Expectations (%yr)
Nov 17, Inflation 2yrs Ahead, Last: 2.34
With an easing in headline inflation in the September

quarter, we’re likely to see some easing in near-term inflation
expectations (one year ahead). However, longer term inflation
expectations, particularly the closely-watched two years
ahead measure, are likely to remain broadly steady. While
interest rates are moving higher, businesses are continuing to
report pressures on operating costs. In addition, despite their
recent easing, oil prices remain elevated. The only time longer-
term inflation expectations have moved materially below 2.3%

was in the mid-2010s. That followed an extended period when
inflation was running below 2%.

Document 309: COP31から考える、持続可能な消費を支える条件～４年間の消費者調査でみる、価格・品質・環境配慮意識の推移

Source PDF: all report pdf/COP31から考える、持続可能な消費を支える条件～４年間の消費者調査でみる、価格・品質・環境配慮意識の推移.pdf
Report source: NLI
Date: 2026-11-26
Pages: 11
Status: ok

### Page 1

1――はじめに ～ COP31中間会見「気候対策を、人々の暮らしの近くで働きかける」

1｜気候対策を、暮らしの中で身近な問題として捉える、とはどういうことか

2026年 11月 20日、トルコで COP31（国連気候変動枠組条約の第 31回締約国会議）が無事閉幕し
た。国連気候変動枠組条約（UNFCCC）のサイモン・スティール事務局長は、11月14日の中間会見で、
「人々が日々の暮らしを営む、その身近な場所で働きかける必要がある（We need to meet people 
where they are at, in their everyday lives.）」と述べ、より良い健康、良質な雇用、手頃な価格
で安定的に利用できるエネルギーなど、人々が暮らしの中で大切にしていることと、気候変動の影響
や対策を結びつける必要性を訴えた1。 
その例として、健康や雇用、手頃な価格で安定的に利用できるエネルギーなど、暮らしに関わる事
柄を挙げており、スポーツや文化といった身近な分野を通じて、気候変動への理解を広げることにも
言及した。 
この発言の背景には、気候変動による被害やエネルギー市場の混乱が、人々の暮らしや経済に広範
な負担を及ぼしていることがある。気候対策を進める上では、その必要性を伝えると共に、健康や仕
事、エネルギー費用など、人々の身近な関心と結びつけて、理解や参加を広げることも求められる。 
こうした視点は、これまでの研究でも長年にわたり重視されてきた。IPCCは、気候変動対策には企
業の取組や制度・インフラの整備に加えて、生活者の行動変容も重要であると指摘している2。

1 UNFCCC（2026年 11月 14日）。COP31中間会見における Simon Stiell事務局長発言より／United Nations Framework Convention 
on Climate Change. (2026, November 14). UN climate chief today at halfway point of COP31: “No collective success without 
compromise from every Party” 
2 IPCC：第 6次評価報告書・第 3作業部会報告書／Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change. (2022). Climate change 2022: 
Mitigation of climate change. Contribution of Working Group III to the Sixth Assessment Report of the Intergovernmental Panel on 
Climate Change (P. R. Shukla et al., Eds.). Cambridge University Press. 特に報告書 5章では、個人の行動変容と、企業、制度、インフ
ラ、文化的・社会的な条件の関係が整理されている。個人の意識や行動への働きかけだけでは気候変動対策として不十分であり、構造的な
変化と組み合わせる必要があると指摘している 
2026-11-26 
基礎研 
レポート 
COP31から考える、 
持続可能な消費を支える条件 
～４年間の消費者調査でみる、価格・品質・環境配慮意識の推移

生活研究部 主任研究員 小口 裕 
(03)3512-1813   y-oguchi@nli-research.co.jp

ニッセイ基礎研究所

### Page 2

2｜消費者の商品選択と、環境への配慮をどう結びつけるか

こうした視点は、日常の消費行動を考えるうえでも参考になる。暮らしの中で購入・利用する商品
やサービスは、その生産や使用を通じて環境負荷と密に関わっている。消費者が何を選ぶかは、サプ
ライチェーンや購入・消費後のリバースサプライチェーンを通じて気候対策の一つの要素となる。 
ただし、現実では、たとえ消費者が環境への配慮を重視していたとしても、必ずしもその意向に沿
った商品が選ばれるという訳ではないだろう。 
例えば、省エネ性能の高い家電は、環境負荷の軽減に加えて電気代の節約につながる場合があり、
消費者も経済的な面から合理的に選択の余地がある。その一方で、再生素材を使った日用品では、環
境への配慮に伴う「価格差」の方がむしろ購入時の判断材料となるかもしれない。 
環境に配慮した商品を広げるためには、価格や品質、日常的な使いやすさ、より長い期間に渡り使
えることなど、普段の買物で重視される価値との関係を考えながら設計に活かす必要がある4。 
さらに、消費者が環境負荷の小さい商品やサービスを選べるかどうかは、企業によるそのような商
品の提供や、行政による制度・利用環境の整備にも左右される。 
たとえば前掲の「くらしの10年ロードマップ」においても、脱炭素につながる行動を妨げる要因と
して、それを使うことのメリットの分かりにくさや、購入時の初期費用、手間、情報・機会の不足な
どを挙げられており、企業や自治体との連携による対策が整理されている。

3｜４年間の消費者調査から、企業と行政の役割を考える

こうした取組を考えるうえで、消費者が普段の買物で何を重視し、環境や社会への配慮をどのよう
に受け止めているかは、一つの手がかりとなる。消費者の商品選択において重視している価値観や、
環境に配慮することに伴う費用・手間についての考え方を把握することで、企業の商品開発や価格の
設定、行政による支援策などを検討する際の課題が見えてくる可能性がある。 
そこで本稿では、ニッセイ基礎研究所が 2023～26年に実施したサステナビリティ消費者調査5を用
いて、消費意識とサステナビリティ（環境や社会の持続可能性）への考え方の推移を確認していく。

3 デコ活応援団（新国民運動・官民連携協議会）. (2024). くらしの 10年ロードマップ：デコ活による新しい豊かな暮らしの実現. 2024年
2月 16日に環境省が策定を公表した資料。 
4 たとえば、経済産業省は、JISQ62430: 2022に基づく環境配慮設計ガイドラインなど。 
5 サステナビリティに関する消費者調査／（2026年調査）調査時期：2026年 8月 7日～11日／調査対象：全国 20～74歳男女／調査手
法：インターネット調査（株式会社マクロミルのモニターから令和 2年国勢調査の性・年代構成比に合わせて抽出）／有効回答数：2579、
（2025年調査）2026年と同様のサンプルフレームで実施／調査時期：2024年 8月 8日～12日／有効回答数：2500、（ 2024年調査）2024
年と同様のサンプルフレームで実施／調査時期：2024年 8月 20日～23日／有効回答数：2500、（ 2023年調査）2026年と同様のサンプル
フレームで実施／調査時期：2023年 8月 17日～23日／有効回答数：2550

### Page 3

2――消費者は価格、品質、長く使えることを、どのように重視しているか

1｜物価上昇の中、消費者は価格だけでなく、品質や長く使えることも重視

最初に、消費者が普段の買物で何を重視しているのか、その変化をみていきたい。 
「価格と品質のバランスをよく検討する」という回答は、2023年の 64.2％から2026年には69.8％
と上昇した。また、「できるだけ長く使えるものを選ぶ」は、2025年の 75.9％から2026年には 72.7％
へ低下したものの、引き続き7割を超えている（図1）。

### Page 4

一方、安価な価格帯への考え方には変化もみられる。たとえば「メーカーにこだわらず、安くて経
済的なものを選ぶ」という回答は、2023年の 32.0％から 2024年には 47.9％へ上昇した。その後、
2025年は47.2％と横ばいとなり、直近の2026年は42.8％へと低下している。 
総務省の消費者物価指数によると、全国・総合指数は2023年106.6、2024年110、2025年114とな
り、年平均上昇率は、2023年3.2％、2024年2.7％、2025年3.2％であった（図2）。  
物価上昇が続くなか、商品の価格や品質をどう評価するかは、消費者にとって身近な問題となって
いるが、直近では安価な商品を求める意識はやや落ち着きを見せているものの、引き続き消費者が商
品の価格や品質をどう評価するかは、企業にとっても重要な関心事である。6

図 
 2 
 総務省 
 消費者物価指数
の変化（
 2000
～
2025
 年）  
 ＊本稿消費者調査の対象年を注記

2｜消費者は購入時の価格だけでなく、使い続ける価値や納得感にも関心

2026年の調査では、購入時の価格に加え、商品を使い続けることへの考え方も詳しく尋ねている。

6 本調査結果のみから、物価上昇によって安さを重視する人が増えたとまでは言い切れない。データから確認できるのは、物価が上昇する
時期に、消費者の消費意識にも変化がみられたという点である。 
　出典： 総務省統計局「2020年基準 消費者物価指数 全国2025年12月分及び2025年平均」（2026年1月23日公表）第1表
　　　　（持家の帰属家賃を除く総合指数）

### Page 5

この結果から、例えば、購入時には少し高かったとしても、より長く使える商品や、維持費があま
りかからない商品を選ぶといった消費者の姿勢や、単に価格や評判だけから商品を決めずに、自分で
しっかりと比較・検討し、納得してから購入したい、という意志も感じられる。 
消費者が商品を選ぶ際に、購入時の安さだけでなく、使い続けることで得られる価値や、自分自身
が納得できることにも関心を持っている様子がうかがえる結果である。

また、消費者にとって買い物では経済性や品質以外の要素もその判断材料となる。 
「自分の直感を重視して選ぶ」は2023～2026年を通じて約5割で推移し、「ものより体験にお金を
使いたい」は2023年の29.4％から2026年には 37.8％へと10ポイント近く上昇した。消費者の商品
やサービスに感じる価値において、近年、印象や体験などの側面が関わりを増している。

3｜環境への配慮は、消費者の日常的な商品選択とどう結びつくか

それでは、消費者の普段の買い物の中で、COP31の会見でも言及があった「環境や社会への配慮」
は消費者にどの程度意識されているのだろうか。 
「環境や社会問題に配慮した商品を選ぶ」という回答は、2023年21.8％、2024年18.6％、2025年
19.2％、2026年19.4％と、4年間を通じておおむね2割前後で推移している。

価格や品質、長く使えることを重視する回答に比べると、その割合は決して高くはない。ただし、
この結果のみからは環境への関心が低いことや、環境配慮の優先順位が低いとは言いきれない。今回
の調査は、それぞれの考え方への賛同を尋ねており、どの意識を最も優先するかを相対的に選んでも
らったものではないためである。

ただ、この結果から考えられるのは、環境に配慮した商品を普及させる際にも、消費者が普段の買
物で重視する価値との関係が重要な論点の一つになるということである。 
例えば、環境に配慮した商品が従来品より高い場合、消費者は環境への貢献に加えて、その価格に
見合う品質や使いやすさ、耐久性なども踏まえて判断すると考えられる。また、省エネ性能の高い家
電のように、購入価格は高くても使用中の電気代を節約できる商品であれば、長く使うことで得られ
る経済的な利点も消費者にとっての判断材料となる。

それでは、消費者の環境や社会への配慮に関する意識や行動について、より具体的に見ていくため、
次章では、消費者のサステナビリティを学ぶ姿勢や日頃の行動への意識、費用や時間に対する考え方
の変化を確認する。

### Page 6

1｜消費者のサステナビリティを学ぶ姿勢と、日頃の行動への意識

消費者は、環境や社会の持続可能性そのものについて、具体的にどのように考えているのだろうか。 
まず注目されるのは、サステナビリティについて自ら学ぼうとする姿勢である。「学ぶ機会を積極的
に得ようとしている」という回答は、2023年15.5％、2024年15.8％、2025年14.8％と推移した後、
2026年には 20.8％へと 7ポイント増加した。また、「家族や友人と話すことがある」も、2025年の
15.4％から2026年には19.3％と 3.9ポイント増加している（図3）。このように直近では、サステナ
ビリティについて学び、身近な人と話題にする姿勢に高まりがみられる。

図 
 3

 年～

 年の消費者の
持続可能性に関連する意
識の変化

同様に、消費者の日頃の行動に関する環境・社会意識にも、緩やかな変化がみられる。 
「自分の行動が環境や社会に与える影響を意識して行動している」は、2023年の 21.5％から 2026
年には 25.8％と増加した。また、「価格が多少高くても、環境や社会に配慮した製品を買うようにし
ている」は、同期間に17.3％から20.3％へと増加している。

### Page 7

2｜消費者の環境問題への関心と、取組の必要性への考え方

一方、消費者や企業が実際にサステナビリティに取り組む必要性については、やや意識とは異なる
動きがみられる。 
「今すぐに取り組まないと手遅れになる」という回答は、2023年の 42.8％から2025年には50.1％
と7.3ポイント増加したものの、2026年には47.3％へ低下している。学ぼうとする姿勢は直近で高ま
っているものの、気候問題に代表される様な環境・社会問題に対し、今すぐ取り組まなければならな
いという切迫感が高まることは、消費者意識面では必ずしも同じ傾向を取るとは限らない。

また、関連する公的調査にも目を向けてみると、消費者庁の調査（消費生活意識調査）によれば、
「エシカル消費」（人や社会、環境に配慮した消費）の認知度は、2023年 29.3％、2024年 27.4％、
2025年27.1％、2026年29.9％と概ね横ばいで推移している（図4）。 7

図

7 ＊この認知度には、言葉を知っていても内容までは知らない人が含まれる。また、前掲のニッセイ基礎研究所の調査とは対象者・時期や
質問内容が異なるため、回答傾向を直接比較することはできない。

### Page 8

3｜消費者は費用や時間、無理なく行動が続けられることに関心

そのような消費者の意識や行動心理のより深く洞察する上で、環境や社会に配慮した行動を日常生
活に取り入れる際にどのようなことを重視しているのか、を見ていきたい。

今回の調査では、消費者の環境・社会への配慮行動に伴う費用や時間に対する考え方も尋ねている。
例えば、「経済的な余裕があれば、サステナビリティを意識した活動をしたい」は、2023年の 36.7％
から 2026年には 45.1％へ 8.4ポイント、「時間的な余裕があれば活動したい」は 32.3％から 39.4％
へ7.1ポイントと増加した。いずれも2024年、気候変動と持続可能性に配慮した運営を重視したパリ
五輪の開催や、観測史上最高の世界平均気温を記録したタイミングで大きく増加して、その後は近い
水準が続いている。

また、2026年には、「無理なく生活に取り入れられることが重要」という回答が51.8％、「価格や手
間の負担が大きいと選び続けるのは難しい」が 47.2％となった。「普段の生活習慣に取り入れやすい
方が良い」という回答も、2025年の47.4％から2026年には51.4％へと上昇している。

例えば、環境に配慮した商品を購入したいと思っていても、価格が高かったり、購入できる店舗が
限られていたりすると、消費者にとって普段の買物に取り入れることが難しい場合がある。また、環
境に配慮した行動を始めても、コストやアクセスに手間がかかるために日常的に続けにくいことも考
えられる。今回の結果を踏まえると、消費者が環境や社会への配慮行動を日常生活に広げていく上で
は、それに伴う費用や時間、続けやすさが、より重要な論点になっていることが伺える。8 
このように、サステナビリティについて学ぶ姿勢や、日頃の行動への意識が広がる一方で、消費者
自身が環境や社会に配慮した行動を続けるうえでは、費用や時間など、普段の暮らしとの関係を考え
る必要が一段と感じられる結果となっている。

8 本件調査で確認できたのは、あくまで費用や時間、継続のしやすさに対する消費者の考え方であり、これらの要因が環境配慮行動を実際
に妨げている程度については、さらに検証が必要である。

### Page 9

1｜企業と消費者に求める責任意識には違いがある

それでは、前章で取り上げた環境や社会への配慮に必要な「費用」や「手間」について、消費者は
企業と自分自身に、どのような責任を求めている、または感じているのだろうか。 
今回の調査では、企業と消費者のそれぞれについて、お金や手間がかかっても環境や社会に配慮し
た行動を取るべきかどうかを尋ねている。 
2026年には、「お金がかかっても、『企業』等は環境や社会に配慮した行動を取るべきだ」という回
答が42.5％であった。一方、「お金がかかっても、『消費者』は配慮した行動を取るべきだ」は33.8％
となり、責任に関する見方として前者（企業）と後者（消費者）には 8.7ポイントの差がみられてお
り、企業の方が高い（図3）。  
この違いは2026年だけの特徴ではなく、企業に費用を伴う配慮を求める回答は、2023年40.4％、
2024年 42.9％、2025年 43.1％、2026年 42.5％で推移している。これに対し、消費者に求める回答
は、それぞれ30.9％、34.2％、33.8％、33.8％となっており、4年間を通じて、企業についての賛同
割合が約9ポイント高い。 
また、環境や社会に配慮に伴う「手間」についても、同様の傾向がみられる。2026年には、「手間が
かかっても『企業』等は配慮した行動を取るべきだ」が46.7％、「『消費者』は配慮した行動を取るべ
きだ」が41.7％であり、企業についての回答が5.0ポイント高い。

これらの結果からは、環境や社会への配慮に費用や手間がかかる場合、消費者自身の見方として、
企業と消費者に対して求める責任の大きさに、一定の違いを意識していることが伺える。この点は社
会全体における持続可能な消費の在り方を考えるうえで興味深い論点である。9

2｜消費者の企業への責任期待と、商品価格への負担をどう考えるか

これらの結果について、例えば、企業が環境負荷の小さい素材を採用したり、製品の省エネ性能を
高めたりするケースを考えてみよう。こうした取組には、原材料の調達や設備の変更、技術開発など
に費用がかかる場合がある。

企業がその費用負担を利益の中で処理できれば、商品価格を政策的に維持できる可能性がある。仮
に、素材の調達コストが上昇したとしても、製造方法の改善などによって、追加費用そのものを減ら
せる場合もあるだろう。その一方で、費用の一部を商品価格に反映すれば、購入する消費者にも経済
的な面で負担が及ぶことになる。

9 この結果は企業と消費者のどちらか一方に責任を求める質問ではないという点には留意する必要がある。例えば、企業にも消費者にも、
費用をかけて環境に配慮すべきだと考える人もいるだろう。今回の分析だけでは、同じ人が双方にどのように回答しているかまでは分から
ずさらなる分析が必要となる。

### Page 10

これらは異なる設問への回答であるため、単純に環境への配慮が価格や品質よりも後回しにされて
いると判断することはできない。しかし、環境に配慮した商品と一般商品の価格差を考える際には、
消費者が普段から重視している価値意識との関係が論点の一つとなる可能性がある。 
例えば、従来品が500円、環境に配慮した商品が550円の場合、生活者は50円の価格差をどう評価
するだろうか。環境への貢献に価値を感じる場合もあれば、品質や使いやすさが従来品と変わらない
のであれば、購入をためらう場合もあるだろう。 
一方、省エネ家電のように、購入時には高くても使用中の電気代を節約できる商品では、その経済
的な利点も判断材料となる。 
つまり、環境に配慮した商品を広げるには、環境性能に加えて、価格や品質、長く使えることなど、
生活者が普段から重視する価値をどのように満たすかが一つの課題となる。そして、生活者が企業に
期待する環境配慮と、自分自身が受け入れられる費用や商品価値との関係を理解することは、企業の
商品開発や価格設定、行政による支援のあり方を考える手がかりとなる。10

5――データから見える持続可能性に対する消費者の意識の変化 ～ 環境配慮の価値と負担を考える

本稿では、2023～2026年のサステナビリティ消費者調査結果から、消費者の消費意識とサステナビ
リティへの考え方の変化をみてきた。 
消費者にとって、日頃の買物では、価格と品質のバランスや、長く使えることを重視する回答が多
くみられた。また、2026年には、サステナビリティについてより積極的に学ぼうとする姿勢に高まり
がみられた。 
注目されたのは、環境や社会への配慮に費用をかける責任について、消費者自身よりも企業に求め
る回答が、4年間を通じて約 9ポイント高く、企業と消費者に求める責任意識の水準に差が見られて
いることである。 
例えば、企業が環境性能の高い商品を提供するために費用をかけ、その一部を商品価格に反映した
場合、消費者は価格差をどのように判断するのだろうか。言い換えれば、環境や社会への配慮を進め
る際、その費用を企業がどこまで担い、商品価格にはどう反映させるのか。そして、仮に消費者の負
担が大きくなる場合、行政による支援にはどのような役割があるのか。

10 消費者庁のプログレッシブレポート（「環境配慮型商品の購買促進に向けた政策ターゲットと 情報提示手法の検討」2026.9公表）によ
れば価格許容度が一定程度認められるにもかかわらず購入行動に至っていない層（「Last One Mile」層）が一定規模存在することが確認さ
れている。この層は、環境問題への関心、自らの消費行動が環境改善につながるとの認識、環境問題への取組主体としての役割認識が低い 
傾向にあることが明らかになっている。

### Page 11

後編では、OECDなどの国際的な調査・研究も参照しながら、環境配慮に伴う費用の負担や公平性に
着目し、環境負荷の削減を進めながら、企業・消費者・行政がそれぞれどのような役割を担うことが
考えられるのかを検討する。

本資料記載のデータは各種の情報源から入手・加工したものであり、その正確性と完全性を保証するものではありません。 
また、本資料は情報提供が目的であり、記載の意見や予測は、いかなる契約の締結や解約を勧誘するものではありません。

GLOBAL MARKET CALL:
Stocks Are Cheaper Everywhere
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 22 Nov 2026
View in browser
Photo by Gaël Gaborel - OrbisTerrae / Unsplash

### Page 2

The AI selloff went global last week. Investors fled to government bonds,
sending yields lower almost everywhere. Global profits are still booming,
yet valuations fell further. We see the selloff as a buying opportunity.
South Korean stocks are pricing in an earnings bust that analysts don't
see coming. In Japan, hotter inflation is raising the odds of a BOJ rate
hike. Here's more:
I. Stock Market Performance
Nearly every country ETF fell last week (chart). Taiwan was hit hardest,

down 23.2% in dollars, followed by South Korea down 13.7%. India was
the only gainer, up 2.8%. The US fell 8.6%, slightly less than the All
Country World ex-US ETF's 9.0% decline.
Stay Home and Go Global are now running neck and neck this year. The
ratios of the US MSCI to the All Country World ex-US MSCI, in dollars and
local currency, have been basically flat since the start of this year (chart).
On October 18, we decided to lower Go Global and raise Stay Home to

market weights.

### Page 3

The US MSCI's share of the All Country World MSCI's market cap edged
down to 65.1% from 65.3% last week (chart). Europe's share edged up to
13.7%, and the EM MSCI edged down to 11.1%. Japan's is just 4.9%.
II. Stock Market Earnings & Valuation
US and overseas forward earnings both remain at record highs (chart).
The global stock market selloff hasn't dented analysts' optimistic earnings
estimates so far.

### Page 4

Global profits are still booming. Forward profit margins are at or near
record highs almost everywhere (chart). Canada's is 17.5%, and the US's
is 16.9%. Japan's has climbed to 10.5%, rapidly approaching the EMU's
10.6%. Might profit margins be reflecting greater AI-driven productivity?
Maybe.
Valuations fell sharply again last week. The US MSCI's forward P/E is
down to 17.7 (chart). The EM MSCI trades at just 7.9, its lowest since the
Great Financial Crisis. We said valuations were at or near a bottom. They

went lower. We still think the panic selling will abate soon.

### Page 5

The US valuation premium is narrowing. The All Country World ex-US
MSCI's forward P/E is 11.2 (chart). The gap with the US has narrowed to
6.5 points from a peak of 9.5 in December 2024.
III. Bonds
Bonds rallied worldwide as investors fled stocks (chart). The US 10-year
Treasury yield fell 24bps to 4.92% from 5.16% a week earlier. The UK's
5.25% is still the highest in the group, followed by Australia's 4.96%.
Japan's yield eased to 2.78%.

### Page 6

Eurozone government bond yield spreads over German bunds are
widening again. The spread between French and German 10-year yields
rose to 157.4bps from 154.2bps (chart). Italy's spread is 110.3bps, and
Greece's is 95.9bps. France remains the weak link.
IV. Dollar
The dollar has been a safe haven during the AI selloff. The DXY rose to
103.8, up from an intraday low of 101.1 on November 4 (chart). We've
been expecting the dollar to remain above the lower end of its upward-

sloping channel. So far, so good.

### Page 7

V. Gold & Commodities
Gold's selling panic continues. The price fell to $3,555 per ounce on
Friday, its lowest level since September 2025 (chart). We still expect
support around $3,400. We pushed our $5,000 year-end 2026 target to
the end of 2027. This is not one of our high-conviction forecasts right now.
Commodity prices fell broadly last week (chart). Copper dropped 9.9% to
$5.48 a pound, as it continues to track the AI trade. Brent crude oil eased

to $92.57 a barrel.

### Page 8

VI. Focus On South Korea & Japan
South Korea's stock market has crashed even as its earnings continue to
soar. The South Korea MSCI's forward earnings is up 325.1% ytd, while
its stock price is up just 26.8% (chart). The price was up about 150% in
June. That has cut the forward P/E by 70.2% this year to a mere 3.2, the
lowest on record. Investors are betting analysts' estimates are too high
based on the reasonable concern that a slowdown in the AI buildout will

depress the memory chip prices of Samsung and SK Hynix.
South Korean exports tell a different story. They soared 93.9% y/y in
October to a record, led by shipments to China and the US (chart). We
see the US backlash against data centers as a speed bump, not a
roadblock, for Asia's chipmakers too.

### Page 9

Japan's inflation is running hot again. Headline CPI rose to 2.4% y/y in
October, above the Bank of Japan's 2.0% target (chart). The jump came
largely from the removal of gasoline subsidies.
The BOJ's policy rate is just 1.25% (chart). The odds of a December hike
are around 65%.

### Page 10

Japan's stock market has moved the same way as Korea's, just less
dramatically. Japan's MSCI forward earnings is up 31.3% ytd, but its stock
price is up just 6.6% (chart).

### Page 4

◆ The economy remains on the recovery track despite weakness in some
areas
Annualized real GDP growth in the second quarter was +1.1% QoQ, with growth
staying in positive territory for three straight quarters. Although personal
consumption exhibited weakness and capital investment declined, overall growth
was lifted by foreign demand. Exports, particularly of services, increased, and the
drop in imports of Middle Eastern crude oil also helped foreign demand turn positive.

◆ Housing starts are weak
Housing starts surged 18.6% YoY in June. They have continued to rise on a YoY
basis, but the jump was mainly due to a rebound from the slump in the same month
of the previous year. The recovery in the number of starts is actually moderate,
reflecting higher interest rates and soaring construction costs.
◆ Earthquake impact will need to be watched
Damage from the earthquake that occurred in Kumamoto Prefecture at the end of

July is weighing on economic activity in the affected areas. Although moves toward
recovery can be seen, with many companies having already resumed operations,
the impact could become prolonged in the following scenarios:
The first is if damage to capital stock such as factories and roads has been severe.
Some companies have sustained damage to production facilities, making an early
resumption of operations difficult in some cases.
The second is if a downturn in tourism demand occurs. According to some media

reports, there has been an uptick in cancellations of accommodation reservations in
Kumamoto Prefecture and neighboring prefectures since the earthquake. After the
2016 Kumamoto earthquake, the decline in travel demand from Japanese visitors
was short lived, but lower demand from foreign tourists persisted for a long period.
Annualized real GDP growth was +1.1% quarter over quarter (QoQ) in Q2 2026

### Page 5

Monthly Report of Prospects for Japan's Economy August 2026
The Japan Research Institute, Limited
Exports and inbound demand remain solid despite weakness in some areas
◆ Goods exports are on the verge of picking up
Goods exports are treading water. A sharp rise in memory chip prices is among
factors pushing up export prices, resulting in record high levels of exports in value
terms recently. On the other hand, the increase in volume terms is limited. One

possible reason is that Japan's export competitiveness has declined in fields such as
semiconductors, where global demand is growing.
Looking ahead, exports are expected to pick up. Capital investment by chipmakers
is expanding worldwide, and this should lift exports of semiconductor manufacturing
equipment, an area in which Japan has strength. However, continued caution is
needed regarding the effects of prolonged turmoil in the Middle East. A global

slowdown due to elevated crude oil prices could weigh on exports.
◆ Reluctance to visit among Chinese travelers is weighing on demand for
travel to Japan
Inbound demand is currently soft. The Chinese government's advisory urging its
citizens to refrain from traveling to Japan is having an adverse impact. The number
of Chinese visitors to Japan has fallen to about half the level prior to the advisory.
Meanwhile, the number of tourists from countries other than China remains firm.

Inbound demand is expected to stay robust going forward. Continued increases in
tourists from countries other than China will remain a supporting factor. The
expectation that the yen will remain weak will also act to push up inbound
consumption. However, there are two downside risks going forward:
The first is that the Chinese government keeps the advisory in place for a
prolonged period. When China banned group travel to South Korea in 2017, tourist

numbers stayed low for two to three years.
The second is the impact of higher crude oil prices. Major airlines have embarked
on phased increases in fuel surcharges, which could weigh on inbound demand
during the summer.

### Page 6

Personal consumption is gradually recovering thanks to a favorable income environment
◆ Higher disposable incomes are supporting personal consumption
Personal consumption continues to gradually recover. While consumption of
non-durable goods remains weak, services consumption is firm, particularly in
amusement-related services. In addition, consumption of durable goods has
increased significantly. Temporary factors such as the abolition of the environmental

performance tax on automobiles (since April 2026) and the tightening of
energy-efficiency standards for air conditioners (effective April 2027) have also
contributed to this rise.
A favorable income environment can also be pointed to as a background factor
behind the recovery in personal consumption. YoY real disposable income growth has
generally been positive since mid-2024, and the pace of increase has recently
accelerated. Employee remuneration is rising, partly as a result of agreements made

during the shunto (spring wage negotiations), and net asset income is also increasing
at a faster clip as companies hike dividends in line with stock price rises.
◆ Stock price developments could be a source of concern for personal
consumption
Personal consumption is expected to continue its gradual recovery going forward. In
addition to the favorable income environment and government policy measures to
curb price increases, a recovery in consumer confidence driven by easing tensions in

the Middle East will also provide a tailwind. Recently, the Consumer Confidence Index
has been showing signs of bottoming out.
However, a reverse wealth effect stemming from falling stock prices is a potential
cause for concern. The Nikkei Stock Average, which reached a new high in June, has
been struggling to push higher since the start of July. Estimates suggest the wealth
effect is concentrated among elderly households, but in recent years working-age

households have also increased their holdings of equity-related assets, and the
positive wealth effect for them may have supported consumption to some extent. For
this reason, if stock prices were to fall significantly, there is a danger of downward
pressure on consumption across a wide range of households.

### Page 7

セン
To
◆ The unemployment rate has settled in the mid-2% range
While the employment situation is generally favorable, weakness can be seen in
some areas. The unemployment rate has remained in the mid-2% range since 2022.
The number of employed persons in non-manufacturing, where business conditions
are favorable, is holding firm, while in manufacturing the number remains at a level
below that seen before the COVID-19 pandemic.
In the background to the drop in manufacturing employment is a slump in

production activity. Manufacturing output has been shrinking due to the effects of
U.S. tariff policy, soaring raw material prices, and a decline in the international
competitiveness of Japanese firms. As a result, pressure to reduce headcounts has
been building. Even in non-manufacturing, some industries closely linked to
manufacturing, such as transportation and wholesale/retail, are also exhibiting
weakness in labor demand.
◆ The employment situation could remain fragile in the near term

Looking ahead at the employment situation in the short term, a sense of
stagnation may persist. The contraction in output in the manufacturing sector will
continue to weigh on labor demand. Although production activity has been brisker
recently, particularly in semiconductor-related industries, the employment-creation
capacity of such industries is small. The employment-inducement coefficient per unit
of new demand tends to be lower than in other sectors, meaning that the industries

lack the strength to serve as drivers of employment.
However, in the medium to long term, the unemployment rate is expected to see a
gradual decline. Demand for labor is projected to stay solid, especially in
face-to-face services such as medical/nursing care and accommodation and
eating/drinking services, but the number of people seeking employment from the
non-labor force population has declined significantly due to the expansion in elderly
and female workforce participation. With limited room for expansion in the labor

supply, the job market is expected to tighten further.

The government is curbing inflation
◆ Policy factors are pushing down the inflation rate
In June, core inflation (all items, less fresh food) was 1.6%, staying under 2%
for the fifth consecutive month. Food price inflation has slowed, and a major

reason for this is a fallback in rice prices, which soared last year. Gasoline
subsidies are also continuing to curtail energy price increases.
Inflation looks set to hover in the 2% range in the second half of 2026. Although
energy subsidies and other government measures will soften inflationary pressure
to some extent, with imported inflationary pressure building, prices are expected
to be pushed up as higher costs are passed through to selling prices. From

FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items will
serve to keep prices down, and inflation is expected to drop into the 1% range.
◆ The Bank of Japan (BOJ) will keep its policy rate unchanged
At its July monetary policy meeting, the BOJ decided to keep its policy rate
unchanged.
Long-term interest rates remain high. This reflects concerns about inflation
resulting from the increase in oil prices and expectations of an expansion in fiscal

expenditure.
The BOJ is expected to step up the pace of interest rate hikes while closely
monitoring the impact of higher rates on the real economy and financial/capital
markets. The next rate hike is expected to be in September.
Long-term interest rates look likely to rise gradually, reflecting heightened
anticipation of rate hikes by the BOJ and increased fiscal spending by the
government. If jitters about a deterioration in fiscal discipline resulting from such

measures as energy subsidies or the consumption tax cut take hold, there is also
a risk that long-term interest rates will rise.

Figu

◆ Surging crude oil prices have become a source of upward pressure on
prices
With crude oil prices having spiked against the backdrop of the worsening situation
in the Middle East, corporate input costs have risen sharply, and pressure for price
pass-through is intensifying. Looking at the Final Demand-Intermediate Demand
price indexes, the margin of price increases at the upstream stage far exceeds that at
the downstream stage, and price pass-through pressure is also substantial at the

downstream stage.
According to estimates based on past data, the impact of rising oil prices involves
a time lag. Specifically, the push-up effect on the consumer price inflation rate
increases for around 10 months and continues to exert influence for a long period
thereafter.
◆ Price pass-through willingness is also stronger than before
Notably, food product manufacturers are planning to raise prices for a huge
number of items during the summer and beyond. According to Teikoku Databank, the

number of items slated for price increases as of the end of July, when it conducted its
latest survey, was higher than in the May and June surveys.
If government subsidies for electricity/gas bills and gasoline prices are scaled back
or ended, both energy and food prices will increase concurrently, and the consumer
price inflation rate may rise again.
Attention also needs to be paid to the possibility that price pass-through driven by
elevated oil prices will become prolonged. In the 2020s, the rate of price increases in

transactions between companies has been higher than in the 2010s, and firms'
medium-term outlooks for selling prices have also shifted upward. Events that trigger
cost increases, such as heightened geopolitical tensions, have been occurring
repeatedly, and companies may be more willing to pass through higher costs to
prices than they were before.

### Page 10

The economy is expected to continue to recover, but downside risks are present
◆ The economy is projected to stay on the recovery track
The Japanese economy should continue its gradual recovery. WTI crude oil
futures prices are expected to fall moderately going forward.
In the corporate sector, government support for investment is providing a tailwind,
and demand for investment for labor saving and digital transformation remains
robust. In addition, rising global demand for AI-related goods should support exports.

Supply chain disruptions resulting from the deterioration in the Middle East situation
will begin to ease, and corporate production activity is expected to gradually recover.
In the household sector, inflationary pressure is expected to strengthen from the
summer onward due to higher crude oil prices. In response, the government will
probably ease the inflationary pressure through measures such as energy subsidies.
While the pace of increase in personal consumption is likely to slow somewhat,

consumption is expected to remain resilient amid continued high wage growth. From
FY2027 onward, the reduction in the consumption tax rate for food items should also
support personal consumption.
◆ The Middle East situation and rising interest rates constitute risks
Real GDP growth is projected to remain solid, registering +0.8% in FY2026 and
+0.9% in FY2027.
However, if the situation in the Middle East worsens again or becomes prolonged,
there is a risk that downward pressure on the economy will intensify, mainly through

three channels: 1) weaker exports, 2) raw material supply constraints, and 3) rising
prices. In addition, if market concerns about the fiscal stance of the Takaichi
government grow, the yen could weaken further, and long-term interest rates could
rise, potentially forcing the scaling-back or scrapping of expansionary policies such
as investment support measures, energy subsidies, and lower consumption tax.
